CLAUDE   2026-11-11 12:58
==============================================================================
Scope for everything below: one design system (Droppy), one app (MealDrop), five tasks, one model (`claude-opus-5` at high effort), 650 runs. The reasons are reconstructed from transcripts, not recorded.

## Clustering results

285 story loads fall into 15 clusters in 6 families. Workflows are `701` new UI, `702` rework UI, `703` fix a bug, `704` fix accessibility, `706` new UI with scheduling. "Stated" counts the calls where the agent said its purpose in the transcript.

| Family (calls, share) | Cluster | What the agent wanted | Calls | Most-loaded story | Workflows | Stated |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| Worked example (113, 40%) | `example-model` | Copy how the design system builds the UI it is about to build | 72 | Button "Mealdrop Checkout Actions" | 702, 704, 706 | 7 |
| | `example-form-wiring` | See whether the in-app example uses a real form, a submit control, native field attributes | 41 | Input "Mealdrop Checkout Form" | 703, 704 | 5 |
| Undocumented contract (69, 24%) | `native-contract` | Confirm a fact the props table omits, from a story named for it | 50 | Button "Test Defaults To Type Button" | 703, 704 | 22 |
| | `indirect-probe` | Dig in an unrelated story for native-attribute hints | 14 | Button "Anatomy" | 703, 704 | 6 |
| | `bounds-behaviour` | Learn what happens at value limits | 5 | QuantityStepper "Bounds Disable Rather Than Clamp" | 702 | 0 |
| Second look (34, 12%) | `reload-isolate` | Re-read one story out of a long docs page | 18 | Button "Test Defaults To Type Button" | 702–704 | 7 |
| | `reload-opaque` | Re-load a story hoping to see behind a helper | 16 | Modal "Empty" | 701, 704 | 0 |
| Component basics (30, 11%) | `parts-wiring` | Learn parts, slots and basic wiring | 14 | QuantityStepper "Anatomy" | 702, 704 | 0 |
| | `variant-catalogue` | Find which visual styles exist | 10 | Button "Variants" | 706 | 0 |
| | `classname-hook` | Check for a styling escape hatch | 3 | Badge "Class Name" | 701 | 0 |
| | `guessed-story` | Asked for a story name it was never shown | 3 | Modal "Open" | 701 | 0 |
| Accessibility (22, 8%) | `a11y-name` | How to name a control, and the wording | 12 | QuantityStepper "aria-label" | 702, 704 | 0 |
| | `a11y-semantics` | What the component announces by itself | 10 | Input "Test Error Is Announced" | 704 | 1 |
| Component choice (17, 6%) | `usage-guidance` | Find when-to-use guidance in the only story | 10 | QuantityStepper "Empty" | 702 | 0 |
| | `example-precedent` | See which component the design system itself uses | 7 | QuantityStepper "Mealdrop Cart Line" | 702 | 0 |

How the clusters distribute:

- **By task:** every family differs significantly by workflow.
  - `703` (fix a bug): 39 of 49 loading runs are after an undocumented contract.
  - `706` (new UI with scheduling): all 29 loading runs load an in-app example.
  - `702` (rework UI): the only workflow with component-choice loads.
- **By docs:** with full docs (`full`), no run loads a story for component basics or accessibility; without docs (`stories-full`), 25 and 10 of 50 runs do. Worked-example and undocumented-contract loads survive docs (26 and 14 of 50 runs).
- **By JSDoc:** stripping it (`purge-jsdoc`) changes nothing measurable.
- **Evidence strength varies by family:** undocumented contract is the best supported (28 of 69 stated). Component basics, accessibility and component choice rest almost entirely on inference (1 stated out of 69 calls combined).

Reliability: three independent open clusterings gave 18, 15 and 18 clusters (adjusted Rand 0.91, 0.61, 0.59). Against the merged codebook, three coders agree on 98.9% of held-out calls (α 0.986). A second annotator's rewrite lands in the same cluster 93–94% of the time.

## Quotable: counts and tests that do not depend on reconstructed reasons

- **Agents rarely load a specific story.** 177 of 650 runs (27%) loaded one; 285 loads in total.
- **Demand is concentrated.** Five stories account for 65% of loads. In every arm, at least 87% of the stories listed to the agent were never loaded.
- **Usage examples are the kind in highest demand.** They are 134 of the 285 loads (47%). Without docs they are loaded on 15.3% of the occasions they are listed, against 2.4–4.5% for test, anatomy and showcase stories and 0.6% for prop stories.
- **Docs reduce story loading.** 32 of 50 runs load a story with full docs, against 45 of 50 without (p = 0.0001); 42 loads against 131.
- **With docs, only two kinds of story are still loaded.** All 42 loads in `full` are usage examples (28) or test stories (14). Anatomy, showcase and prop stories are never loaded there.
- **JSDoc makes no measurable difference** to story loading (36 against 32 of 50 runs, p = 0.31).
- **A story named for a fact gets found.** "Test Defaults To Type Button" was loaded in 10 of 10 bug-fix runs in three arms.
- **Examples that hide their markup are dead ends.** 63 of 134 usage-example loads returned a one-line call to a demo helper.
- **Stories are consulted while planning.** 272 of 285 loads (95%) happen before the first edit.
- **Agents rarely guess names.** 3 of 285 loads asked for a story never shown.

## Quotable with attribution

These rest on LLM-reconstructed reasons, so say so when quoting.

- **Reason shares:** 40% worked example, 24% undocumented contract, 12% second look, 11% component basics, 8% accessibility, 6% component choice.
- **Docs remove the "basics" and accessibility reasons.** This one is corroborated by the deterministic story-kind result above.
- **The pull of in-app examples is unchanged by docs.** About 80% of loading runs load one in all three comparable arms.
- **The task decides the need.** Quote it as that, not as a rule about workflow types.

## Not quotable

- **Anything about payoff.** The "what did the agent do with it" judgement has 56% agreement between two annotators. That rules out "0 of 72 example loads were adopted" and "47% gave no new information".
- **"Stories make agents better" or "valuable" in an outcome sense.** This measures what agents reach for, not whether it helped.
- **Generalisations by workflow type**, such as "bug fixes need contract stories". There are five tasks, one per workflow.
- **Any `purge-docgen` comparison.** That arm inlines every story, so its loads are all re-loads.
- **The 98.9% agreement as proof the reasons are right.** It measures consistency between Claude coders, and the codebook decides first by story kind.
- **"Story kind predicts reason"** (Cramér's V 0.60, 72%). It is partly built into the codebook.
- **Specifics of small clusters** (`bounds-behaviour`, `classname-hook`, `guessed-story`, `example-precedent`) and of arms with 13 loads or fewer.
- **The low rate for default and prop stories as "unwanted".** They are usually among the three stories already inlined.
- **The replay pilot numbers.** Seven of eight self-reports matched, but that is eight truncated answers; the "60 tokens of reasoning per step" comes from three calls.
- **The exact percentages as general constants.** Outside this experiment, quote direction and rough size only.

      python3 - <<'EOF'
      import json,collections,random,re,os
      recs=[json.loads(l) for l in open('story-loads.final.jsonl')]
      runs=[json.loads(l) for l in open('exposures.jsonl')]
      allruns=json.load(open(os.path.expanduser('/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-steve--claude-spares-spare-NvhUUY/705b61eb-04e9-4095-83d1-75d2dc67e898/scratchpad/runs.json')))
      print('stored run dirs',len(allruns),'| collected',sum(r['collected'] for r in allruns),'| current',len(runs),'| current with MCP',sum(r['arm']!='control-none' for r in runs),'| arms with MCP',len({r['arm'] for r in runs if r['arm']!='control-none'}))
      loaders={r['run_key'] for r in recs}
      print('runs with a load',len(loaders),'of MCP runs %.1f%%'%(100*len(loaders)/sum(r['arm']!='control-none' for r in runs)))
      # regrouping
      def regroup(r):
          c=r['cluster']
          if c in('example-form-wiring','parts-wiring'): return 'wiring (form + component)'
          if c=='classname-hook': return 'undocumented-contract'
          if c=='example-model': return 'worked-example'
          return r['cluster_family']
      g=collections.Counter(regroup(r) for r in recs)
      gr=collections.defaultdict(set)
      for r in recs: gr[regroup(r)].add(r['run_key'])
      print('\nREGROUPED: group | calls | share | runs')
      for k,v in g.most_common(): print('  %-28s %3d  %4.1f%%  %3d'%(k,v,100*v/len(recs),len(gr[k])))
      arms3=('stories-full','full','purge-jsdoc')
      print('  wiring by arm (calls):',dict(collections.Counter(r['arm'] for r in recs if regroup(r).startswith('wiring')).most_common()))
      print('  wiring split: form',sum(r['cluster']=='example-form-wiring' for r in recs),'component',sum(r['cluster']=='parts-wiring' for r in recs))
      # name-only exposure
      exp=collections.Counter((r['exposure_before'], bool(r['listing_description'])) for r in recs)
      print('\nexposure x has-description',dict(exp))
      # quotes naming the story
      def norm(s): return re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9 ]+',' ',s.lower())
      q=[r for r in recs if r.get('evidence_quote')]
      named=[r for r in q if norm(r['story_name']) in norm(r['evidence_quote'])]
      print('calls with a quote',len(q),'| quote contains the story name verbatim',len(named), '| by evidence',dict(collections.Counter(r['evidence'] for r in named)))
      for r in named[:4]: print('   e.g.',r['story_name'],'<-',r['evidence_quote'][:150])
      # items for name-vs-need coding (no cue, no cluster)
      rnd=random.Random(7); order=recs[:]; rnd.shuffle(order)
      items={}; key={}
      for n,r in enumerate(order,1):
          i='n%03d'%n
          items[i]={'component':r['component'],'story_name':r['story_name'],'listed_description':r['listing_description'],'need':r['need']}
          key[i]=r['call_id']
      json.dump(items,open('clustering/name-items.json','w'),indent=1); json.dump(key,open('clustering/name-key.json','w'))
      print('\nitems',len(items),os.path.getsize('clustering/name-items.json'),'bytes')

  [tool] Bash: Compute coder agreement on name alignment and break the counts down by evidence, exposure, story kind and arm
      python3 - <<'EOF'
      import json,collections,itertools
      key=json.load(open('clustering/name-key.json'))
      cod=[json.load(open('clustering/name-coded-%d.json'%i))['assignments'] for i in (1,2,3)]
      items=sorted(key)
      pairs=[sum(a[i]==b[i] for i in items)/len(items) for a,b in itertools.combinations(cod,2)]
      unan=sum(len({c[i] for c in cod})==1 for i in items)/len(items)
      # Krippendorff alpha nominal
      def alpha(labs):
          m=len(labs); o=collections.Counter()
          for i in items:
              v=[l[i] for l in labs]
              for x,y in itertools.permutations(range(m),2): o[(v[x],v[y])]+=1/(m-1)
          nc=collections.Counter()
          for (c,_),v in o.items(): nc[c]+=v
          n=sum(nc.values()); do=sum(v for (c,k),v in o.items() if c!=k); de=sum(nc[c]*nc[k] for c in nc for k in nc if c!=k)/(n-1)
          return 1-do/de
      print('pairwise agreement',['%.1f%%'%(100*p) for p in pairs],'unanimous %.1f%%'%(100*unan),'alpha %.3f'%alpha(cod))
      recs={r['call_id']:r for r in map(json.loads,open('story-loads.final.jsonl'))}
      lab={}
      for i in items:
          v=collections.Counter(c[i] for c in cod).most_common(1)[0]
          lab[key[i]]=v[0] if v[1]>=2 else 'unresolved'
      n=len(lab); c=collections.Counter(lab.values())
      print('ALL LOADS',dict(c),'| aligned (direct+context) %d = %.1f%%'%(c['direct']+c['context'],100*(c['direct']+c['context'])/n))
      def brk(title,f):
          print(title)
          g=collections.defaultdict(collections.Counter)
          for cid,l in lab.items(): g[f(recs[cid])][l]+=1
          for k,v in sorted(g.items(),key=lambda kv:-sum(kv[1].values())):
              t=sum(v.values()); print('   %-22s n=%3d direct %3d context %3d none %3d | aligned %.0f%%'%(k,t,v['direct'],v['context'],v['none'],100*(v['direct']+v['context'])/t))
      brk('by evidence',lambda r:r['evidence'])
      brk('by what the agent had seen',lambda r:{'named_in_docs':'listed','named_in_listing':'listed','inlined':'already shown','unseen':'guessed'}[r['exposure_before']])
      brk('by story kind',lambda r:r['story_kind'])
      brk('by arm (3 comparable)',lambda r:r['arm'] if r['arm'] in('stories-full','full','purge-jsdoc') else 'other arms')
      # listed-only subset
      ls=[cid for cid in lab if recs[cid]['exposure_before'] in('named_in_docs','named_in_listing')]
      cl=collections.Counter(lab[c] for c in ls); print('LISTED-ONLY loads n=%d'%len(ls),dict(cl),'aligned %.1f%%'%(100*(cl['direct']+cl['context'])/len(ls)))
      # distinct stories
      st=collections.defaultdict(collections.Counter)
      for cid,l in lab.items(): st[(recs[cid]['component'],recs[cid]['story_name'])][l]+=1
      maj={k:v.most_common(1)[0][0] for k,v in st.items()}
      print('DISTINCT STORIES',len(st),dict(collections.Counter(maj.values())))
      for l in('direct','context','none'):
          top=sorted(((sum(v.values()),k) for k,v in st.items() if maj[k]==l),reverse=True)[:6]
          print('  top',l,[('%s "%s"'%k,n_) for n_,k in top])
      json.dump(lab,open('clustering/name-alignment.json','w'),indent=1)
      EOF

CLAUDE   2026-11-11 13:58
==============================================================================
In 254 of 285 story loads (89%), the story's name matched what the agent was looking for. Three coders judged each load independently and agree on 99.6% of them.

Each load got one of three labels:

- **Direct:** the name states the information sought, such as "Test Defaults To Type Button" for whether a Button submits a form.
- **Context:** the name names the UI the agent is building, such as "Mealdrop Checkout Form" while fixing the checkout form.
- **None:** the name does not point at the need; the story was a fallback or the only one available.

| | Loads | Direct | Context | None | Name matches the need |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| All loads | 285 | 120 | 134 | 31 | 89% |
| Story only seen in a list beforehand | 239 | 105 | 120 | 14 | 94% |
| Agent stated its purpose | 48 | 31 | 12 | 5 | 90% |
| Story already shown in full | 43 | 12 | 14 | 17 | 60% |

Counted by story instead of by load: 35 distinct stories were loaded, and 27 have a name that matches the need they were loaded for.

## What you can tell readers

- **The name is usually all the agent has.** 239 of 285 loads (84%) were of a story the agent had only seen in a list: 159 by name alone, 80 with a one-line description.
- **When an agent picks a story from a list, the name matches its need 94% of the time.** About half name the exact information and half name the UI being built.
- **Agents say so themselves.** In 17 of the 48 loads where the agent stated its purpose, it quoted the story's name as the reason.
- **Without a well-named story, agents grope.** "Test Defaults To Type Button" was loaded 55 times. The 14 loads where agents probed an unrelated story for the same kind of fact were mostly in arms that do not list it: Button "Anatomy" accounts for 10 of them.
- **The misses are generic names.** The 31 "none" loads are mostly "Anatomy" and the autogenerated "Empty" stories.

## Cautions before quoting

- **Part of the match is built in.** The needs were reconstructed by an annotator who could see the story's name. The cleaner figure is the stated-purpose row: 43 of 48 (90%).
- **This counts loads, not a controlled comparison.** It shows that loaded stories had matching names, not that renaming a story would get it loaded.
- **A matching name is not enough.** 63 of the 134 "context" loads returned a one-line helper call instead of markup.
- **The agreement figure is high because the label is almost a property of the name.** Every usage example came out "context" and every test story "direct".

The label is in the `name_alignment` column of `story-loads.csv`, and the report has a new "Story names" section.

  [tool] Agent (general-purpose): Code cited reasons, coder 1
      You are one of three independent coders. Work alone: read only the file named below and do not look for other coders' output.
  
      File: /Users/steve/Development/storybook/agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/clustering/cited-items.json (small; read all of it).
  
      Each item is one sentence a coding agent wrote just before loading one Storybook story of a design-system component. Fields: `component`, `story_loaded` (the story's title), `agent_said` (the agent's own words). In none of these does the agent quote the story's exact title.
  
      For each item, label the REASON the agent itself gives for loading the story. Use exactly one label, applying this order of precedence (take the first that fits):
  
      1. `decision-hinges`: the agent says a design or implementation decision depends on the answer, or that it wants to verify something before assuming or deciding (e.g. "this decides whether…", "before assuming…", "before deciding how to…", "to verify … before designing the fix").
      2. `docs-gap`: the agent cites something missing from, or surprising in, the documented props or docs as the reason (e.g. "the documented props have no `type`").
      3. `sweep`: the agent says it is going through the remaining or other stories or docs in case something is documented there, without pointing at a particular story.
      4. `points-at-story`: the agent simply points at the story or kind of story it is about to load, by topic or paraphrase (e.g. "the cart-line story", "that story", "the checkout-specific stories"), giving no further reason.

  [tool] Bash: Write the pie-chart script and generate the SVG files
      mkdir -p /Users/steve/Development/storybook/agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts && cat > /Users/steve/Development/storybook/agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/scripts/make_pies.py <<'EOF'
      """Standalone SVG pie charts for the story-load analysis.
  
      Plain SVG, no CSS or scripts: every slice is a <path id="slice-..."> and every label is live <text>,
      so the files open cleanly in Figma, Illustrator or Inkscape. Colours are the Okabe-Ito set without
      yellow, ordered so the two weakest pairs never sit next to each other; validated for all pairs under
      protan, deutan and tritan vision (worst pair dE 7.6, which is why every slice also has a direct label).
      """
      import collections, glob, json, math, os, re
      from xml.sax.saxutils import escape
  
      OUT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
      PALETTE = ['#0072B2', '#E69F00', '#009E73', '#56B4E9', '#CC79A7', '#D55E00']
      INK, MUTED, FONT = '#1f1f1e', '#5c5b56', "Inter, 'Helvetica Neue', Arial, sans-serif"
      W, CX, CY, R = 880, 440, 250, 140
  
      def slug(s):
          return re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9]+', '-', s.lower()).strip('-')
  
      def pie(filename, title, subtitle, slices, caption=()):
          """slices: [(label, count)] in drawing order, clockwise from 12 o'clock."""
          total = sum(n for _, n in slices)
          height = 470 + (26 + 19 * len(caption) if caption else 0)
          out = ['<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 %d %d" width="%d" height="%d" font-family="%s" role="img">' % (W, height, W, height, FONT),
                 '<title>%s</title>' % escape(title),
                 '<desc>%s</desc>' % escape('; '.join('%s %d (%d%%)' % (l, n, round(100 * n / total)) for l, n in slices)),
                 '<rect id="background" width="%d" height="%d" fill="#ffffff"/>' % (W, height),
                 '<text id="title" x="40" y="44" font-size="20" font-weight="600" fill="%s">%s</text>' % (INK, escape(title)),
                 '<text id="subtitle" x="40" y="68" font-size="13.5" fill="%s">%s</text>' % (MUTED, escape(subtitle)),
                 '<g id="slices" stroke="#ffffff" stroke-width="2" stroke-linejoin="round">']
          angle, labels = -math.pi / 2, []
          for k, (label, n) in enumerate(slices):
              sweep = 2 * math.pi * n / total
              a0, a1 = angle, angle + sweep
              x0, y0, x1, y1 = CX + R * math.cos(a0), CY + R * math.sin(a0), CX + R * math.cos(a1), CY + R * math.sin(a1)
              d = 'M%.1f,%.1f L%.2f,%.2f A%d,%d 0 %d 1 %.2f,%.2f Z' % (CX, CY, x0, y0, R, R, 1 if sweep > math.pi else 0, x1, y1)
              pct = round(100 * n / total)
              out.append('<path id="slice-%s" d="%s" fill="%s"><title>%s: %d of %d (%d%%)</title></path>' % (slug(label), d, PALETTE[k], escape(label), n, total, pct))
              labels.append({'label': label, 'n': n, 'pct': pct, 'mid': (a0 + a1) / 2, 'color': PALETTE[k]})
              angle = a1
          out.append('</g>')
  
          for side in (1, -1):
              group = sorted((l for l in labels if (math.cos(l['mid']) >= 0) == (side == 1)), key=lambda l: math.sin(l['mid']))
              ys = [CY + (R + 30) * math.sin(l['

CLAUDE   2026-11-11 14:23
==============================================================================
The four pie charts are in `agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts/` as plain SVG: [reason-families.svg](agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts/reason-families.svg), [what-agent-had-seen.svg](agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts/what-agent-had-seen.svg), [name-alignment.svg](agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts/name-alignment.svg) and [cited-reasons.svg](agent-eval/comparisons/story-load-reasons/charts/cited-reasons.svg). The wiring merge is undone and the className hook stays under undocumented contract; the report and its statistics now use that grouping.

## Reason shares

| Family | Loads | Share |
|---|---:|---:|
| Worked example | 113 | 40% |
| Undocumented contract (with className hook) | 72 | 25% |
| Second look | 34 | 12% |
| Component basics | 27 | 9% |
| Accessibility | 22 | 8% |
| Component choice | 17 | 6% |

## The remaining 16%

46 of the 285 loads were not picked from a name in a list.

- **43 re-loaded a story whose full source was already on screen:**
  - 24 are in `purge-docgen`, whose docs output prints every story.
  - 13 are the single autogenerated "Empty" story in `empty`.
  - 6 are a story among the three inlined ones in other arms.
- **3 asked for a name the agent was never shown** (Modal "Open"). All three failed because the story does not exist.

The 43 re-loads have three reasons: isolating one story from a long page (18), trying to see behind a helper (16), and looking for usage guidance in the only story (9).

## How names conveyed information

The chart shows three slices: the name names the UI being built (134, 47%), the name states the information sought (120, 42%), and the name does not match (31, 11%). The caption inside the SVG reads:

> In 89% of loads the story name pointed at what the agent needed. It either named the piece of UI the agent was building ("Mealdrop Checkout Form") or stated the information it was after ("Test Defaults To Type Button"). The rest were generic names such as "Anatomy" or "Empty", loaded as a fallback. Needs were reconstructed by an annotator who could see the story name, so part of this match is built in.

## Other cited reasons

In 48 loads the agent stated its purpose. Three coders labelled the 31 that do not quote the story's exact name (94–97% pairwise agreement).

| What the agent cited | Loads | Share |
|---|---:|---:|
| The story's exact name | 17 | 35% |
| The story by topic or kind ("the cart-line story") | 14 | 29% |
| A decision that hinges on the answer ("this decides whether a real submit button is possible") | 8 | 17% |
| A sweep of the remaining stories ("in case native passthrough is documented anywhere") | 4 | 8% |
| A gap in the documented props ("Button's documented props have no `type`") | 3 | 6% |
| Other | 2 | 4% |

So 31 of 48 stated reasons point at the story itself, by name or by topic. This sample is narrow: 37 of the 48 come from one workflow (`703`), and 28 concern one story, "Test Defaults To Type Button". I would not quote these shares as general.

## About the charts

- **Colours:** the Okabe–Ito set without yellow, ordered so the two weakest pairs never sit next to each other. A validator passed it for every pair under protan, deutan and tritan vision. The weakest pair (pink and green) is only moderately separated, so every slice also has a direct label.
- **Editing:** no CSS or scripts. Each slice is a `<path id="slice-…">`, labels are live text, and the white background is a single `<rect id="background">` you can delete.
- **Form:** pies read poorly when slices are close, such as the two largest in the cited-reasons chart, so the counts are printed on every label.

They are generated by `scripts/make_pies.py`, so they redraw if the data changes.

      """Render this Claude Code session as a readable, shareable text file.
  
      Keeps every message the user typed and every reply in full, plus each tool call and a capped view of
      its output. Drops harness-injected context (system reminders, skill instruction bodies, nudges) and
      hidden reasoning. Redacts email addresses and anything shaped like an API key.
      """
      import json, re, sys
  
      SRC, DEST = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
      INPUT_CAP, RESULT_CAP = 3000, 1500
      QUIET_TOOLS = ('update_plan', 'wrap_up', 'mark_chapter')
  
      def clean(text):
          text = re.sub(r']*>', '', text or '')
          text = re.sub(r'[\w.+-]+@[\w-]+\.[\w.-]+', '[email redacted]', text)
          text = re.sub(r'sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}', '[key redacted]', text)
          return text.strip()
  
      def cap(text, limit):
          text = clean(text)
          return text if len(text) <= limit else text[:limit].rstrip() + '\n[... %d more characters not shown]' % (len(text) - limit)
  
      def indent(text, pad='    '):
          return '\n'.join(pad + line if line else '' for line in text.split('\n'))
  
      def result_text(block):
          c = block.get('content')
          if isinstance(c, str):
              return c
          parts = []
          for part in c or []:
              if isinstance(part, dict):
                  parts.append(part.get('text', '') if part.get('type') == 'text' else '[%s]' % part.get('type'))
          return '\n'.join(parts)
  
      def describe_call(name, i):
          short = name.split('__')[-1]
          if short == 'Bash':
              return 'Bash: %s\n%s' % (i.get('description', ''), indent(cap(i.get('command', ''), INPUT_CAP)))
          if short == 'Read':
              return 'Read: %s' % i.get('file_path', '')
          if short == 'Write':
              return 'Write: %s\n%s' % (i.get('file_path', ''), indent(cap(i.get('content', ''), INPUT_CAP)))
          if short == 'Edit':
              return 'Edit: %s\n%s\n    -->\n%s' % (i.get('file_path', ''), indent(cap(i.get('old_string', ''), INPUT_CAP // 2)), indent(cap(i.get('new_string', ''), INPUT_CAP // 2)))
          if short == 'Agent':
              return 'Agent (%s%s): %s\n%s' % (i.get('subagent_type', 'general-purpose'), ', model ' + i['model'] if i.get('model') else '', i.get('description', ''), indent(cap(i.get('prompt', ''), INPUT_CAP)))
          if short == 'Skill':
              return 'Skill: %s' % i.get('skill', '')
          if short == 'update_plan':
              if i.get('items'):
                  return 'Plan:\n' + indent('\n'.join('[%s] %s' % ({'completed': 'x', 'in_progress': '>'}.get(it.get('status'), ' '), it.get('content', '')) for it in i['items']))
              return None
          if short == 'wrap_up':
              return 'Status recap: %s%s' % (i.get('recap', ''), '\n    Question to user: ' + i['ask'] if i.get('ask') else '')
          return '%s: %s' % (short, cap(json.dumps(i, ensure_ascii=False), 700))
  
      out, names, counts = [], {}, {'user': 0, 'assistant': 0, 'tool': 0, 'subagent': 0}
      RULE = '=' * 78
  
      def emit(header, body, stamp=''):
          out.append('\n%s\n%s%s\n%s\n%s' % (RU
      [... 4021 more characters not shown]

      M='/Users/steve/.claude/projects/-Users-steve-Development-storybook/705b61eb-04e9-4095-83d1-75d2dc67e898.jsonl'
      c=collections.Counter(); ids=set(); dup=0; sample=[]
      for line in open(M):
          try:o=json.loads(line)
          except Exception: continue
          m=o.get('message')

          if isinstance(value, str):
